---
id: T20
title: ENV fail-open: ENV must be explicit; development-mode reset and SQL echo must not be reachable by default
depends:[]
platform_issue: 75
---

## Metadata

- **Phase**: phase-5-audit
- **Type**: security-audit
- **Area**: audit-batch
- **Size**: M
- **Milestone**: Security audit remediation (operator directive 2026-09-13)
- **Priority**: high

## Reason (audit provenance)

SEC AUDIT HIGH (A-3, D-2): with ENV unset one staff token wiped the store via /admin/reset and rewrote the PIN to the published 1234; echo=True leaked PII by the same default. Severity HIGH.

## Acceptance

app refuses to start with ENV unset (no silent development default) OR defaults to a non-development value; /admin/reset 403s unless ENV=development is set EXPLICITLY; SQLAlchemy echo never enabled by default; /health env field does not reflect an implicit default; tests pin all three

## Files

backend/app/config.py backend/app/main.py backend/app/database.py backend/tests/

## Context

Related: Platform #75. Audit sources: `audit-A-out/report.md` A-3 ("`ENV` defaults to `development`,
and in that state a staff token can wipe the store and reset the staff PIN to the published 1234")
and `audit-D-out/report.md` D-2 (an independent lane reproducing the same defect: `/health`
answering `env=development` implicitly, `POST /api/v1/admin/reset -> 204`, and 138 SQLAlchemy echo
lines carrying a guest name and phone on stdout).

The whole defect is one defaulted field and the three places that read it. All four are measured in
"Groom baseline" below:

- `backend/app/config.py:36` - `env: str = Field(default="development", ...)`. The permissive value
  is the default, so "the operator forgot one environment variable" is a sufficient precondition for
  dev mode, and nothing validates the value either (`ENV=staging` is accepted verbatim).
- `backend/app/routers/admin.py:638` - `if get_settings().env != "development": 403`, then
  `seed.seed_data(reset=True)`, which drops and re-creates the whole schema. The guard is a string
  equality against a value whose default is the permissive side.
- `backend/app/database.py:42` and `backend/app/seed.py:35` - `echo=settings.env == "development"` on
  the application engine and on the seeder's engine. Both write every bound parameter, including
  guest names and phone numbers, to the process log.
- `backend/app/main.py:513` - `/health` reports `settings.env` to an unauthenticated caller, so the
  implicit dev state is externally confirmable.

`app/seed.py` also writes `hash_pin("1234")` unconditionally - the credential-rollback half of A-3.
That literal and the seed path's env reach belong to #64 (T9, in flight) and #78 (T23, seed and
fixture hygiene); see Out of scope. What this issue owns is that the reset *endpoint* cannot be armed
by default.

- Design: not a UI issue, so no `_docs/design-system.md` anchor.
- Plan: `_docs/plan.md` (B-01 config contract, B-12 reset contract).

## Decision (PM, T20 groom round 1)

Four questions had to be settled before any AC could be falsifiable. They are settled here, and the
ACs pin the chosen *behaviour*, never the chosen implementation.

**D-1 - explicit-ENV-required, not default-production.** `Settings` refuses construction when `ENV`
is absent and when it carries a value outside the allow-list `development | test | production`.
Chosen over `default="production"` for three reasons. (1) Precedent: this same class already refuses
construction for a weak `jwt_secret` (T10's quality gate is on main), and A-1's fix direction asks for
the two in one breath ("require an explicit `ENV` value ... so 'unset' is never a production state").
(2) A `production` default is only safe for today's three consumers; the next
`if settings.env == "development"` (docs surface per A-10/D-10, traceback verbosity per D-3, some
future debug route) re-opens exactly this hole with a default that now lies the other way and a store
that silently behaves as though nothing was defaulted. Fail-fast is operator-visible at boot; a
silent default is invisible in precisely the way the audit calls the defect. (3) The AC pair is
stronger: with a required field, AC-1's refusal clauses and AC-3's "never answers" clause measure one
mechanism, whereas a `production` default would leave every guard still keyed on a value nobody chose.
The cost is stated honestly in Constraints: every run command that omits `ENV` breaks loudly, which is
the point, and it is a documented contract change (`README.md:275` lists `ENV` as optional with
default `development`).

A `settings.env_explicit`-style companion attribute is *not* the mechanism and no AC may require one:
pydantic-settings v2 folds `os.environ` and constructor kwargs into one validated namespace, so a
default-applied field and a value that came from `ENV=...` are indistinguishable on the instance.
Measured at base with the probes named in Groom baseline: `model_fields_set` on an instance built with
no `env` kwarg and no `ENV` variable is `['database_url','jwt_secret','staff_pin']` - no `env` - while
with `ENV=production` exported it becomes `['database_url','env','jwt_secret','staff_pin']`. So the
only reliable signal is presence at *construction* time, which is why the check is a validator that
fires on a value the caller never supplied rather than an attribute reporting where a value came from.

**D-2 - the reset guard keeps reading the resolved setting, and only `development` passes.**
`get_settings().env` stays the read seam (B-12's contract, and `test_admin_reset.py:143` documents
why), and the comparison stays an equality against the single permissive value: `development` passes,
`test` and `production` answer 403, anything else never reaches the handler because D-1 refused the
boot. AC-2 additionally pins that the endpoint does not answer 204 for a process whose environment
merely *omitted* the word. No second opt-in variable (`ALLOW_DATA_RESET`) is required here: a second
flag whose default is also a default is the same class of bug one level down, and #78 (T23) owns the
seed-literal half of that destructive surface. If a second gate is ever added it must not weaken the
env check.

**D-3 - `echo` is decoupled from `env` and defaults off; T21 owns the logger, T20 owns the
derivation.** Neither `backend/app/database.py:42` nor `backend/app/seed.py:35` may compute echo from
`settings.env`. The field is named `SQL_ECHO` (AC-5 pins the name, because an AC cannot assert against
a name it does not state), and the hard rule is that echo is off unless it is explicitly asked for.
The split, stated once: **T20 owns the echo default coupling only** - the `echo=` keyword on those two
`create_engine` calls and the settings field that replaces the `env ==` expression. **T21 owns logger
configuration** - handler, formatter, level, uvicorn's duplicate ASGI traceback, traceback verbosity,
and D-3's `[SQL: ...]`-inside-an-exception-message leak, which travels the logging path and is not an
`echo=` leak. T20 adds no `log_config` and no handler.

**D-4 - `/health` keeps its `env` field and must never name an environment the operator did not
choose.** The field stays: `test_health.py` asserts its presence, and A-11's trim-to-`{"status":"ok"}`
proposal is out of scope here. Under D-1 there is no implicit value left to report, so AC-3 pins the
externally visible half: a process booted with `ENV=production` reports exactly `production`, a
process booted with no `ENV` never answers at all, and the field never carries an off-list value.

## Groom baseline (measured at base 1fc1521 in this worktree)

Gate env for every measurement below: `TZ=UTC`, `PYTHONPATH=$PWD/backend`,
`backend/.venv -> /home/te/tq/suite/backend/.venv`, `STAFF_PIN=0000`, and a `JWT_SECRET` of at least
32 non-published characters. T10's secret-quality gate is merged, so a short or published secret no
longer boots at all - read `backend/app/config.py`'s validator before writing any block that boots the
app, and note that `backend/tests/conftest.py` on main already carries a gate-compliant test secret.
`ENV` is named explicitly in every command that is not *about* `ENV` being absent.

1. Settings construction with no `ENV` anywhere (no `.env` file consulted, no kwarg):
   `K1_from_os_environ: 'development'` with `ENVIRON_HAS_ENV: False None` - the field resolves to the
   permissive value silently - and `K4_arbitrary_kwarg_accepted: 'staging'`, i.e. no allow-list. The
   `model_fields_set` reading D-1 leans on is quoted there.
2. Whole-app boot with `env -u ENV` and a token minted with the process's own `JWT_SECRET` (what a
   logged-in staff member holds), one process, `TestClient`:
   `MAIN_ENGINE_ECHO: True` / `SEED_ENGINE_ECHO: True` /
   `HEALTH: {"status":"ok","version":"0.1.0","env":"development"}` / `RESET: 204`,
   and the seeder's engine wrote its bound parameters to the process log, guest rows included
   (`... 'Alice', '0900-000-001', 2, 'window seat' ... 'Henry', '0900-000-008' ...`). A-3 and D-2
   reproduced in this worktree: one POST, one unset variable.
3. Controls. Same boot with `ENV=production`: `/health` reports `production`, both engines are off,
   the reset answers 403 (D-2's control, reproduced). Same boot with the default flipped to `test`:
   `MAIN_ENGINE_ECHO: False`, `HEALTH: ... "env":"test"`, `RESET: 403` - which is why the default flip
   alone is a mutant AC-1 must reject rather than a fix.
4. Red-baseline counts for the suites this round touches, so the SW round can tell its own damage from
   inherited noise. Measured in this worktree by `_t20/probes/baseline_counts.py`, which runs each
   file in a hand-built environment that supplies every gate variable and pops `ENV` (so the number
   describes the harness as this issue sees it, not as an interactive shell happens to have it):
   `test_config.py` 1 passed, `test_health.py` 1, `test_admin_reset.py` 9, `test_seed.py` 15,
   `test_startup.py` 1, `test_startup_bootstrap_settings.py` 1 - all exit 0, 0 failed, 0 errors -
   and `backend/tests` whole: **272 passed, 2 xfailed, 0 failed** in 78s. Zero failures at base is
   the point: any failure after the fix is the fix's own damage, and the whole-suite number is the
   floor quoted in Definition of Done.

## Acceptance criteria

Five blocks, each runnable on its own from the repo root of the tree under test. Do not run them under
`set -e` or `set -o pipefail`, and never pipe one into a truncating consumer such as `head`: a
state-changing block that is SIGPIPEd yields a false FAIL, and rc is not the verdict here in either
direction - the verdict TOKEN is (`PASS AC-<n>:` for the whole-AC line, `FAIL AC-<n> <clause>: <what
was measured>` per clause). This is a FIX issue, so every block is expected RED at the groom base
through at least one of its own clauses, and each block's `Measured today:` line carries the raw red
output plus the mutation-falsification arms that were actually measured.

Three conventions that are load-bearing, all measured rather than assumed:

- **Which tree gets imported is stated, not inherited.** `backend/.venv` is a symlink onto the suite
  venv, whose `pyvenv.cfg` makes a child process resolve `sys.path[0]` to the *main checkout's*
  `backend/`, so a block relying on the ambient `PYTHONPATH` can measure another tree and never
  notice. The blocks export `T20PROBEPATH=$PWD/backend` and hand that value down as the child's
  `PYTHONPATH`; AC-5 additionally prints `DB_FILE:`, the resolved `app/database.py` path, so its
  measurement names the tree it came from. That print is the only one of its kind among the five
  blocks, and it is quoted in AC-5's `Measured today:` line; the other four blocks name their tree
  by construction (`T20PROBEPATH=$PWD/backend`, and the scratch directory's absolute path appears in
  each probe's `DATABASE_URL`) rather than by echoing a resolved module path, so AC-5's `DB_FILE:` is
  the line to re-read if a run and the committed red baseline disagree about which tree was measured.
- **Scratch.** Every block creates its own `mktemp -d` directory, points `TMPDIR` at it, and writes
  everything there - staged probes, SQLite files, `__pycache__` included - so a run dirties nothing
  under `backend/` and `git status --porcelain` stays empty, which is the cleanliness check that
  matters for the repo. What a block does *not* do is delete that directory: an earlier draft ended
  each block with `rm -rf "$scratch"`, and it was taken out deliberately, because the staged probe is
  the only copy of the program that produced the numbers being reviewed and a block that destroys it
  cannot be re-inspected after the fact. The scratch directory therefore survives the run (its path
  is in the block's own `DATABASE_URL`/`DB_FILE:` output) and is cleaned up outside the block, in the
  caller's own working area, not in the repo. Run the blocks one at a time: two blocks booting the
  same store fight over it.
- **The environment is a process fact.** `Settings` is `lru_cache`d and both `app.main` and
  `app.database` read it at import time, so every block that measures a boot-time decision does it in
  a fresh subprocess with an environment it builds itself (`ENV` present, or deliberately absent),
  never by monkeypatching a module global. The rebind-the-seam shape is already covered by
  `backend/tests/test_admin_reset.py`, and repeating it here would pin a different fact than the one
  this issue is about.

- [ ] **AC-1** `ENV` is required and validated at construction: a process with no `ENV` set, with
`ENV` set but empty, or with `ENV` outside the allow-list `development | test | production` never
boots the application at all (no silent `development`), while each of the three allowed values boots
and `/health` reports it verbatim.

Pass: the block prints a line beginning `PASS AC-1:`. Its six clauses are named in its own output
(`p_absent_refused`, `p_blank_refused`, `p_staging_refused`, `p_dev_boots`, `p_test_boots`,
`p_prod_boots`) so QA can see which half moved. The three refusal clauses are this AC's red half at
the groom base; the three boot clauses are not-breaking pins, expected GREEN before and after the fix,
because requiring the variable must not break the environments that do name themselves.

Mutation-falsification design, each one edit and each a *different* wrong answer. (a) Flip the default
to `test` *and* add the allow-list (`env: Literal[...] = Field(default="test")`, validator deleted) -
the single edit most likely to be offered as "the fail-open is closed": measured against that tree the
block prints `FAIL AC-1 p_absent_refused: ... saw: HEALTH: ... "env":"test"` and nothing else, so AC-1
is still not satisfied while every other signal looks healthy, and a block that measured only boot
behaviour would wrongly pass. (b) Add the allow-list without the
requirement (`env: Literal["development","test","production"] = Field(default="development")`):
measured at base, `p_blank_refused` and `p_staging_refused` go PASS and `p_absent_refused` alone stays
FAIL, so a fix must refuse the *absence* and not merely the bad value. (c) The reverse mutant - a
validator that rejects an absent `ENV` but admits `staging` - is caught by `p_staging_refused`, which
is why that arm is a refusal clause.

```bash
AC-1 stage steps begin here: replay_block.py reads this line
- Probes: probes/probe1.py
- Probes: probes/clauses1.py
# AC-1 executes: bash, from the repo root of the tree under test. The two probe files
# above are staged by printf of a quoted line list (T10's staged-probe convention, kept in pure bash so this file's fences stay simple). The probe measures and prints labelled lines; the clause table turns them into one verdict per clause plus a final whole-AC token line. Scratch is _t20_scratch, removed on the way out. rc is diagnostic only - the printed token is the contract.
rm -rf _t20_scratch
test -f backend/app/config.py || { echo "FAIL AC-1: run this block from the repo root of the tree under test"; exit 0; }
test -x backend/.venv/bin/python || { echo "FAIL AC-1: backend/.venv is not linked in this worktree"; exit 0; }
mkdir -p _t20_scratch
T20G=0
export TMPDIR="$PWD/_t20_scratch" TZ=UTC
export T20PROBEPATH="$PWD/backend"
export PYTHONPATH="$T20PROBEPATH"
test -n "$T20PROBEPATH" || { echo "FAIL AC-1: T20PROBEPATH is empty, so the probe could not be told which tree to import"; exit 0; }
export STAFF_PIN=0000
export JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac1aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-1 probe: the environment is never resolved implicitly.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Boots the shipped application in a subprocess once per ENV arm (a subprocess is the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'only honest harness here: app.config.get_settings is lru_cached and app.main plus' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'app.database read settings at import time, so the boot-time decision is a process' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'fact, not a per-request one), and prints one labelled line per measurement.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import os' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import subprocess' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm line delimiter, assembled from a character rather than spelled: a literal pipe may' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# not appear in a staged probe, because the tools that slice blocks out of the issue pair' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# fence markers and split arm lines on that character. probe9.py checks that the bytes a' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# block stages are exactly these bytes, so the check has to deny itself too.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm label delimiter, spelled so that neither this source nor its staged copy in the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# issue ever carries a literal pipe: generate_t20_probes.py substitutes the character.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from pathlib import Path' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT = HERE / "run"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD = """' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import os, sys' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    from app.main import app' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except Exception as exc:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("BOOT:", type(exc).__name__, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    raise SystemExit(0)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("BOOT", flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from fastapi.testclient import TestClient' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("HEALTH:", TestClient(app).get("/health").text, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD_FILE = OUT / "child.py"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD_FILE.write_text(CHILD)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'BASE = dict(' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    PYTHONPATH=os.environ["T20PROBEPATH"],' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    TMPDIR=str(OUT),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    TZ="UTC",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    STAFF_PIN="0000",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac1aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' ')' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def run(name, env_extra):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '                         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '    print("ARM %s " + V + " %s" % (name, " ; ".join(lines) if lines else "NO VERDICT LINE"), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if db.exists():' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        db.unlink()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("absent", {})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("blank", {"ENV": ""})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("staging", {"ENV": "staging"})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("dev", {"ENV": "development"})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("test", {"ENV": "test"})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'run("prod", {"ENV": "production"})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/probe.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/probe.py never reached disk"

   printf '%s\n' '"""AC-1 clause table: reads the probe'"'"'s labelled lines, prints one verdict per clause."""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_staging_refused", refused("staging"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "with ENV=staging outside the allow-list Settings refuses construction (want BOOT: ValidationError, saw: %s)" % (arm("staging") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_dev_boots", booted_says("dev", "development"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "with ENV=development the app boots and /health reports development (saw: %s)" % (arm("dev") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_test_boots", booted_says("test", "test"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_prod_boots", booted_says("prod", "production"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "with ENV=production the app boots and /health reports production (saw: %s)" % (arm("prod") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' ']' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'green = True' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'details = []' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '          + V.join(("development", "test", "production"))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          + ", and boots with /health reporting exactly the explicit value for each of the "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            "three allowed environments")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'print("\n".join(details))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

```
 Measured today (this block, run as-is at groom base 1fc1521; first line `FAIL AC-1: at least one
  clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)`):
  `FAIL AC-1 p_absent_refused: ... saw: HEALTH: {"status":"ok","version":"0.1.0","env":"development"}`
  - a process with no `ENV` in its environment boots and calls itself development;
  `FAIL AC-1 p_blank_refused: ... "env":""` - an empty `ENV` is accepted verbatim;
  `FAIL AC-1 p_staging_refused: ... "env":"staging"` - nothing validates the value; with
  `PASS AC-1 p_dev_boots`, `PASS AC-1 p_test_boots`, `PASS AC-1 p_prod_boots` as today's green pins.
  A red block exiting 0 is the correct shape: the token is the contract, rc is diagnostic.
  Green-shape rehearsal, measured against a tree carrying
  `env: Literal["development", "test", "production"] = Field(...)` plus a construction-time validator:
  the block prints `PASS AC-1: Settings refuses construction when ENV is absent, empty or outside
  development|test|production, ...` with all six clauses green.

- [ ] **AC-2** `/api/v1/admin/reset` answers 403 and rewrites nothing unless the process was booted
with `ENV` **explicitly** `development`: the implicit-default path (no `ENV` in the environment) must
die, `test` and `production` keep answering 403, and the documented development reset still works
exactly where it is documented.

Pass: the block prints `PASS AC-2:`. Clauses: `p_absent_reset_403`, `p_test_reset_403`,
`p_prod_reset_403`, `p_dev_reset_reachable`. The store is fingerprinted before and after the single
POST as `<waitlist rows>/<staff_pin_hash prefix>/<max updated_at>`, so "touched nothing" and "wiped
the store and rolled the credential back to the published fixture PIN" are both *measured* rather than
inferred from a status code. That is the A-3 damage model (schema rewrite plus credential rollback),
and it is what makes `p_dev_reset_reachable` a statement about a working reset rather than a count of
204s.

Why per-arm subprocesses and not one process with a rebound settings seam: the fact being pinned is the
*resolution* of `ENV` at boot (`lru_cache`, plus import-time reads in `app.main` and `app.database`),
and `backend/tests/test_admin_reset.py` already owns the rebind shape. Repeating that shape here would
pin the guard's string comparison while leaving the fail-open default untested. The token is minted
through the shipped `jose` path with the process's own `JWT_SECRET` - precisely what a successful login
hands out - so this AC does not fold T10's secret gate into its own verdict.

Mutation-falsification design. (a) The one-line default flip `default="test"` *passes* this AC -
measured against that tree: `PASS AC-2:` with `ARM absent | ... RESET: 403` and a `COUNT_AFTER`
byte-identical to its `COUNT_BEFORE` - and it is explicitly not an acceptable fix, because the endpoint
is then safe only while nobody exports `ENV=development`. AC-1 is what refuses it; the pairing is
load-bearing and QA must read both blocks, not one. (This is the one place where a green block is not
a green issue, and it is recorded here rather than hidden.) (b) Deleting the guard reddens `p_test_reset_403` and `p_prod_reset_403`, the
control arms that prove the guard is not merely broken. (c) A guard inverted to 403 `development` too
reddens `p_dev_reset_reachable`. (d) A handler that answers 403 *after* calling `seed_data` is caught
by the fingerprints, not the status codes: all three refusal clauses require `COUNT_BEFORE` and
`COUNT_AFTER` to be identical strings.

```bash
first=:

   printf '%s\n' 'import os' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '# fence markers and split arm lines on that character. probe9.py checks that the bytes a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# block stages are exactly these bytes, so the check has to deny itself too.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'lines = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'arms = {}' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for ln in lines.splitlines():' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if ln.startswith("ARM "):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        name, _, obs = ln[4:].partition(" " + V + " ")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        arms[name.strip()] = obs' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def field(name, key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for chunk in arms.get(name, "").split(" ; "):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if chunk.startswith(key + ":"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            return chunk.split(":", 1)[1].strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def changed(name):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """True: the store really was rewritten. False: untouched. None: could not tell."""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    before, after = field(name, "COUNT_BEFORE"), field(name, "COUNT_AFTER")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if "/" not in before or "/" not in after or "ERR" in before or "ERR" in after:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return None' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    rows_b, rows_a = before.split("/")[0], after.split("/")[0]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    try:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_prod_reset_403", untouchable("production"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
```

- [ ] **AC-3** `/health` keeps its `env` field and reports exactly the explicit `ENV` value; with no
`ENV` the process never boots, so no implicit environment is ever externally confirmable.

Pass: the block prints `PASS AC-3:`. Clauses: `p_absent_never_answers`, `p_dev_reports_dev`,
`p_test_reports_test`, `p_prod_reports_prod`. `backend/tests/test_health.py`'s existing assertion that
the body carries an `env` string stays true, so this AC is a not-breaking pin on the field's presence
and a red pin on the implicit value. A-11's "trim `/health` to `{"status":"ok"}`" proposal is out of
scope (Out of scope names the issue that owns it).

Mutation-falsification design. (a) Dropping the `env` key from the response (the A-11 direction)
reddens all three boot clauses - the field has to stay. (b) Hard-coding `"env": "production"` while
still requiring the variable reddens `p_dev_reports_dev`: that clause is the control proving the field
*tracks* the explicit value rather than replacing one default with another. (c) Leaving the default in
place reddens `p_absent_never_answers`, the clause carrying this AC's own red.

```bash
   printf '%s\n' ']' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
```

- [ ] **AC-5** SQL statement echo is never armed by the environment label: both engines
(`app/database.py`'s application engine and `app/seed.py`'s seeder engine) are off by default in
`development`, `test`, `production` *and* in a process with no `ENV`, and echo arms only on an
explicit operator request. One real guest join on an explicit-development store writes zero
SQLAlchemy echo lines and zero bound-PII lines to the process log.

Pass: the block prints `PASS AC-5:`. Clauses: `p_default_off_env_absent`,
`p_default_off_in_development`, `p_default_off_in_test`, `p_default_off_in_production`,
`p_explicit_true_arms`, `p_explicit_false_off`, `p_no_pii_in_default_log`. Two properties are required
on purpose and neither alone is enough: the default is off under every label (the coupling is gone),
and an explicit request still arms **both** engines (so the flag is a real setting and not a deleted
line). The no-`ENV` clause deliberately accepts either outcome D-1 permits - a refused boot, or a boot
with echo off - because AC-1 owns the refusal and this block must not double-book it; what it refuses
to accept is a boot with echo armed.

The variable this AC requires is `SQL_ECHO` - a `Settings` field, so `.env`, an exported variable and a
test's explicit kwarg all reach it through the same validated door. Any other name is a contract change
these ACs do not authorise, and an engine that reads `os.environ["SQL_ECHO"]` at build time instead of
the setting fails the kwarg route that `backend/tests/test_admin_reset.py`'s scratch fixture uses.
`p_explicit_true_arms` is the clause that makes a hard-wired `echo=False` impossible, and
`p_no_pii_in_default_log` is the end-to-end half: D-2 counted 138 echo lines on a live server, and this
block counts the same class of lines produced by a real `POST /api/v1/branches/1/waitlist` whose bound
parameters carry a canary name and phone.

Scope split (D-3 above): T20 owns the `echo=` derivation on those two `create_engine` calls and the
settings field replacing it; T21 owns logger handlers, levels, uvicorn's duplicate ASGI traceback and
traceback verbosity, including D-3's `[SQL: ...]`-inside-an-exception-message leak, which travels the
logging path and is not an `echo=` leak. This AC adds no `log_config` and no handler.

Mutation-falsification design, both arms measured. (a) Land D-1 but leave the coupling: a tree carrying
the explicit-ENV validator with `echo=settings.env == "development"` on both engines prints
`FAIL AC-5 p_default_off_in_development: ... MAIN_ECHO: True ; SEED_ECHO: True`, so a round that ships
AC-1 and claims AC-5 is refused. (b) Decouple with `echo=False` hard-wired and no flag:
`FAIL AC-5 p_explicit_true_arms: ... prod=False/ dev=False/`, i.e. the operator's request died together
with the coupling. (c) Wire `SQL_ECHO` into the application engine but forget the seeder's own engine:
the same clause's `SEED_ECHO` half catches it, which is why both engines are printed and both are
required in every clause. (d) Arm echo when `ENV` is *absent* (a debug-by-default reflex):
`p_default_off_env_absent`. Green-shape rehearsal: a tree with `sql_echo: bool = Field(default=False)`
and `echo=get_settings().sql_echo` on both engines prints all seven PASS lines and `PASS AC-5: ...`.
Measured cross-check: that same tree, with D-1's validator added but the two `echo=` keywords left on
`settings.env == "development"`, prints `PASS AC-1:` and `FAIL AC-5:` - the two ACs are genuinely
independent, and shipping one cannot be mistaken for shipping the other.

```bash
AC-5 stage steps begin here: replay_block.py reads this line
- Probes: probes/probe5.py
- Probes: probes/clauses5.py
# AC-5 executes: bash, from the repo root of the tree under test. The two probe files
# above are staged by printf of a quoted line list (T10's staged-probe convention, kept in pure bash so this file's fences stay simple). The probe measures and prints labelled lines; the clause table turns them into one verdict per clause plus a final whole-AC token line. Scratch is _t20_scratch, removed on the way out. rc is diagnostic only - the printed token is the contract.
rm -rf _t20_scratch
test -f backend/app/config.py || { echo "FAIL AC-5: run this block from the repo root of the tree under test"; exit 0; }
test -x backend/.venv/bin/python || { echo "FAIL AC-5: backend/.venv is not linked in this worktree"; exit 0; }
mkdir -p _t20_scratch
T20G=0
export TMPDIR="$PWD/_t20_scratch" TZ=UTC
export T20PROBEPATH="$PWD/backend"
export PYTHONPATH="$T20PROBEPATH"
test -n "$T20PROBEPATH" || { echo "FAIL AC-5: T20PROBEPATH is empty, so the probe could not be told which tree to import"; exit 0; }
export STAFF_PIN=0000
export JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac5aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-5 probe: statement echo is never armed by the environment label.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'echo=True is how D-2 got guest names and phone numbers onto stdout: database.py:42 and' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'seed.py:35 both compute it from settings.env == "development". This probe measures the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'engine'"'"'s own echo flag in a fresh process per arm (import-time state), across the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'cartesian product of {no ENV, development, test, production} x {no SQL_ECHO, SQL_ECHO' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'true/false}, plus one end-to-end arm that serves a real guest join through the shipped' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'app and counts the sqlalchemy echo lines the request wrote to the process log.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Expected truth table: echo True ONLY when the operator explicitly asked for it; the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'environment label never contributes.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import os' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import subprocess' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm line delimiter, assembled from a character rather than spelled: a literal pipe may' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# not appear in a staged probe, because the tools that slice blocks out of the issue pair' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# fence markers and split arm lines on that character. probe9.py checks that the bytes a' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# block stages are exactly these bytes, so the check has to deny itself too.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm label delimiter, spelled so that neither this source nor its staged copy in the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# issue ever carries a literal pipe: generate_t20_probes.py substitutes the character.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from pathlib import Path' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT = HERE / "run"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'DB = OUT / "ac5.db"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD = """' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import os' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    import app.database' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except Exception as exc:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("BOOT:", type(exc).__name__, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    raise SystemExit(0)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("DB_FILE:", app.database.__file__, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("MAIN_ECHO:", app.database.engine.echo, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    from app.seed import get_engine' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except Exception as exc:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("SEED_ECHO: BOOT_" + type(exc).__name__, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    raise SystemExit(0)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("SEED_ECHO:", get_engine().echo, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD_FILE = OUT / "child5.py"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHILD_FILE.write_text(CHILD)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'BASE = dict(' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    PYTHONPATH=os.environ["T20PROBEPATH"],' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    TMPDIR=str(OUT),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    TZ="UTC",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    STAFF_PIN="0000",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac5aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa",' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    DATABASE_URL="sqlite:///" + str(DB),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' ')' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def run(name, extra):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    env = dict(BASE)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    env.update(extra)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    res = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(CHILD_FILE)], env=env,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    obs = [ln for ln in res.stdout.splitlines()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           if ln.startswith(("BOOT:", "MAIN_ECHO:", "SEED_ECHO:"))]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    paths = [ln for ln in res.stdout.splitlines() if ln.startswith("DB_FILE:")]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if paths:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        obs.append(paths[0])' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("ARM %s " + V + " %s" % (name, " ; ".join(obs) if obs else "NO VERDICT LINE"), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def truthy(v):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return str(v).strip().lower() in ("1", "true", "yes", "on")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for envname in (None, "development", "test", "production"):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for echo in (None, "true", "false"):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        extra = {}' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if envname:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            extra["ENV"] = envname' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if echo is not None:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            extra["SQL_ECHO"] = echo' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        run("env=%s;echo=%s" % (envname, echo), extra)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# end-to-end arm: a guest join through the shipped app, with the ENV that used to arm echo.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'JOIN = """' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import os' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from app.seed import seed_data' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'seed_data(reset=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from fastapi.testclient import TestClient' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from app.main import app' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'c = TestClient(app)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'r = c.post("/api/v1/branches/1/waitlist", json={' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    "name": "T20PIICanaryName", "phone": "0977788899", "party_size": 2,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    "note": "canary"})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("JOIN:", r.status_code, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'JOIN_FILE = OUT / "join5.py"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'JOIN_FILE.write_text(JOIN)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'env = dict(BASE, ENV="development")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'res = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(JOIN_FILE)], env=env,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                     capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'joined = [ln for ln in res.stdout.splitlines() if ln.startswith("JOIN:")]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'log = res.stderr + "\n" + res.stdout' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'echo_lines = [ln for ln in log.splitlines() if "sqlalchemy.engine" in ln]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'canary = [ln for ln in log.splitlines() if "T20PIICanaryName" in ln or "0977788899" in ln]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("ARM join-development " + V + " %s ; ECHO_LINES: %d ; PII_LINES: %d" % (' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    " ; ".join(joined) or "NO JOIN LINE", len(echo_lines), len(canary)), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if DB.exists():' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    DB.unlink()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/probe.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/probe.py never reached disk"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-5 clause table: echo is explicit-or-off, and the label never arms it.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Reads the probe'"'"'s labelled ARM lines. Two shapes of evidence are required, and both are' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'stated in the clauses: the DEFAULT is off in every environment label (so the' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'echo=settings.env == "development" coupling is gone), and an EXPLICIT operator request' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'still arms both engines (so the flag is a real setting and not a deleted line). The' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'default arm with no ENV is deliberately satisfied by either outcome T20 D-1 permits - a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'refused boot, or a boot with echo off - because this AC owns the echo coupling and AC-1' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'owns the refusal; requiring the refusal here would double-book it.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm line delimiter, assembled from a character rather than spelled: a literal pipe may' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# not appear in a staged probe, because the tools that slice blocks out of the issue pair' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# fence markers and split arm lines on that character. probe9.py checks that the bytes a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# block stages are exactly these bytes, so the check has to deny itself too.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'lines = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'arms = {}' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for ln in lines.splitlines():' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if ln.startswith("ARM "):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        name, _, obs = ln[4:].partition(" " + V + " ")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        arms[name.strip()] = obs' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def field(name, key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for chunk in arms.get(name, "").split(" ; "):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if chunk.startswith(key + ":"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            return chunk.split(":", 1)[1].strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def num(name, key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    try:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return int(field(name, key))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    except ValueError:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return None' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def booted(name):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """The engine imported. A refusal shows up inside the flag as BOOT_ValidationError."""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return "BOOT_" not in arms.get(name, "") and "MAIN_ECHO:" in arms.get(name, "")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def echo_on(name):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return field(name, "MAIN_ECHO") == "True" or field(name, "SEED_ECHO") == "True"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def echo_off(name):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return booted(name) and not echo_on(name)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'clauses = [' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_default_off_env_absent", (not booted("env=None;echo=None")) or echo_off("env=None;echo=None"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "no ENV and no echo request: the process must refuse to boot (AC-1'"'"'s D-1) or boot with BOTH engines off; it must not boot with echo armed (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("env=None;echo=None") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_default_off_in_development", echo_off("env=development;echo=None"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "ENV=development alone must NOT arm echo - the coupling this issue exists to break (want MAIN_ECHO False SEED_ECHO False) (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("env=development;echo=None") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_default_off_in_test", echo_off("env=test;echo=None"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "ENV=test with no echo request: both engines off (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("env=test;echo=None") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_default_off_in_production", echo_off("env=production;echo=None"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "ENV=production with no echo request: both engines off (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("env=production;echo=None") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_explicit_true_arms", echo_on("env=production;echo=true") and echo_on("env=development;echo=true"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "SQL_ECHO=true MUST arm BOTH engines in every environment, so the flag is a real setting and not a deleted line (want MAIN_ECHO True and SEED_ECHO True in the production and development echo=true arms) (saw: prod=%s/%s dev=%s/%s)" % (' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '         field("env=production;echo=true", "MAIN_ECHO"), field("env=production;echo=true", "SEED_ECHO"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '         field("env=development;echo=true", "MAIN_ECHO"), field("env=development;echo=true", "SEED_ECHO"))),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_explicit_false_off", echo_off("env=development;echo=false"),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "SQL_ECHO=false in development stays off (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("env=development;echo=false") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("p_no_pii_in_default_log", num("join-development", "ECHO_LINES") == 0 and num("join-development", "PII_LINES") == 0,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "one real guest join on ENV=development with no echo request writes zero sqlalchemy echo lines and zero PII lines to the process log (want ECHO_LINES 0 and PII_LINES 0) (saw: %s)" % (arms.get("join-development") or "ARM MISSING")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' ']' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'ok = True' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'details = []' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for name, good, detail in clauses:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if good:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        details.append("PASS AC-5 %s" % name)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    else:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        ok = False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        details.append("FAIL AC-5 %s: %s" % (name, detail))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if ok:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("PASS AC-5: engine echo is decoupled from the environment label and off by default on both engines "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "(app/database.py and app/seed.py), arms only on an explicit request, and the default process log "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "carries no bound guest PII")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("FAIL AC-5: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("\n".join(details))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/clauses.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/clauses.py never reached disk"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/probe.py" > "$TMPDIR/out.txt" 2>&1; RC=$?
sed -e "/sqlalchemy.engine/d" -e "/StarletteDeprecation/d" -e "/^  from starlette$/d" "$TMPDIR/out.txt"
```

 Measured today (this block, run as-is at groom base 1fc1521; 13 ARM lines plus 7 clause lines, first
  line `FAIL AC-5: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)`):
  `FAIL AC-5 p_default_off_env_absent: ... saw: MAIN_ECHO: True ; SEED_ECHO: True ;

  `FAIL AC-5 p_default_off_in_development: ... MAIN_ECHO: True ; SEED_ECHO: True`,
  `FAIL AC-5 p_explicit_true_arms: ... saw: prod=False/False dev=True/True` - `SQL_ECHO=true` does
  nothing under `production` and only appears to work under `development`, and purely because of the
  label,
  `FAIL AC-5 p_explicit_false_off: ... MAIN_ECHO: True ; SEED_ECHO: True`,
  `FAIL AC-5 p_no_pii_in_default_log: ... saw: JOIN: 201 ; ECHO_LINES: 138 ; PII_LINES: 1` - one guest
  join wrote 138 SQLAlchemy echo lines to the process log and one of them carried the canary name and
  phone. `PASS AC-5 p_default_off_in_test` and `PASS AC-5 p_default_off_in_production` are today's green
  control (the coupling only arms under `development`); a correct fix keeps them green and takes the
  other five with them.

- [ ] **AC-4** The suite states its own `ENV` and is repaired by the fix rather than broken by it:
every test-harness module that boots the application carries an explicit `ENV` statement, and that
statement alone is enough to boot the app under the required-`ENV` contract, resolve an allowed
environment, and leave SQL echo off.

Pass: the block prints `PASS AC-4:`. One clause per harness module, named by file
(`conftest.py`, `public_fixtures.py`, `test_public_board.py`, `test_public_waitlist.py`,
`test_staff_dashboard.py`, `test_staff_tables.py`, `test_seed.py`, `_atomicity_probe.py`). A clause
passes when the module's *own* `os.environ.setdefault` statements - scraped from the shipped file,
not invented by the probe - boot `app.main` in a child process that received no `ENV` from anywhere
else, and the resulting settings resolve an allowed environment with `engine.echo` off.

Why the harness is measured and not assumed: six modules above their imports currently carry
`setdefault("ENV", "development")` (`conftest.py`, `public_fixtures.py`, `test_public_board.py`,
`test_public_waitlist.py`) or `"test"` (`test_staff_dashboard.py`, `test_staff_tables.py`,
`_atomicity_probe.py`), and `test_seed.py` carries none at all. Under D-1, "which environment does
this suite run in" stops being an ambient accident and becomes a property of those statements, so an
AC-4 that only re-ran the suite would be satisfied by a suite that still resolves development and
still writes guest SQL to the test log. The child's `DATABASE_URL` is the scratch file (shipped
`sqlite:///./test.db` is relative to the *child's* cwd and would otherwise point at a different
store), and `APP_DIR` is exported so the staged copy resolves `_docs/specs.md` - which
`app/errors.py` reads at import time by walking up from `__file__` - in the tree under test rather
than in the scratch directory.

`JWT_SECRET` is reported by this probe but not gated by it: the published test literals the older
modules carry are T10's contract and #77's work, and folding that verdict in here would make AC-4
unreadable while #77 is in flight. The ARM lines print `JWT_SECRET_LEN` and
`JWT_SECRET_PUBLISHED` so QA sees both facts in one place.

Mutation-falsification design (both arms measured at groom base). (a) Land D-1 without touching the
suite: a tree carrying the required-`ENV` validator and the decoupled echo default, with the shipped
harness statements untouched, prints `FAIL AC-4 conftest.py: ... boot=REFUSED ... stated_env=test`
for every module - the round's own blast radius caught, exactly as A-4 describes it ("a fix that only
edits config.py leaves the suite in the same broken state as AC-1's blast radius, so it must touch
these files or CI fails at import"). (b) The reverse mutant - a harness whose statement is renamed or
deleted while everything else is correct - is caught because the probe scrapes the statements rather
than setting them: the child then has no `ENV` at all and prints `boot=REFUSED`. (c) A harness that
states `development` and therefore boots with echo armed is refused by the `main_echo` term even
though it boots, which is what ties this AC to AC-5 instead of leaving it a boot smoke test.
Green-shape rehearsal: the same block against a tree with the required-`ENV` gate plus
`echo=get_settings().sql_echo` on both engines prints `PASS AC-4` for every module except
`test_seed.py` (the no-statement module AC-4 exists to have repaired), i.e. seven green and one
named-red rather than eight red.

 Measured today (this block, run as-is at groom base 1fc1521; first line `FAIL AC-4: at least one
  harness module fails its clause (8 measured)`):
  `FAIL AC-4 conftest.py: ... saw boot=ok resolved_env=development main_echo=True stated_env=development`
  and the same three-term shape for `public_fixtures.py`, `test_public_board.py` and
  `test_public_waitlist.py` - four harness modules boot the application *as development* with SQL
  echo armed, so today's own test run writes bound guest SQL to the pytest log;
  `FAIL AC-4 test_seed.py: ... boot=REFUSED ... stated_env=<absent>` - that module states no
  environment at all and is the one the fix has to repair. `PASS AC-4 test_staff_dashboard.py`,
  `PASS AC-4 test_staff_tables.py` and `PASS AC-4 _atomicity_probe.py` are today's green control:
  three modules already state `test` and are expected to stay green, which is what proves the block
  can distinguish a repaired harness from an untouched one.

- [ ] **AC-9** Round 1 wrote this AC against a four-block issue and never committed its block, so
the check existed only as a clause list and a hand-pinned digest table. Round 2 commits it, and
widens it to every block the file carries: each recorded block's staged payload must hash to its
recorded digest, every canonical probe source must hash to its recorded digest, the block-to-probe
pairing must be complete in both directions, and the generator must be present.

Pass: the block prints a line beginning `PASS AC-9:`. Clauses: `issue_readable`, one
`payload_ac-<n>` per recorded block, `no_unrecorded_block`, one `source_<file>` per recorded probe,
`no_unrecorded_source`, `probe_clause_pairing`, `generator_present`.

The wording is deliberately about **provenance** rather than about ENV, so no product change can
move it and a documentation-only commit can. Round 1's Constraints recorded the gap in its own
shortfall 5 ("neither is wired to an AC here, so a hand-edit to a block would be caught only by
someone thinking to run them"), and this block is the wiring: a fix round that edits a block's
`printf` lines without regenerating and re-recording gets a named FAIL rather than a quiet
disagreement between the file and the table.

Three properties of the committed checker are part of the contract, because each is a place a
later round could quietly lose the guarantee:

- **A block may not contain a code fence.** A staged probe holding a line that begins with three
  backticks would drop an unbalanced fence into this file and break every tool that pairs markers
  to slice blocks out. So the block stages python only through `printf` of a quoted line list, and
  `stage()` substitutes a `FENCE_MARK` / `PIPE_MARK` token for those literals in any source that
  names them; the probe sources themselves assemble the markers from `chr(96) * 3` / `chr(124)`.
  This AC's own block is the reason AC-13 imports rather than executes it.
- **A payload digest is defined in exactly one place.** `probe9.py` exports `payload_digest()` and
  `record_t20_digests.py` calls it, so the recorder and the checker cannot define "the payload"
  differently - the precise disagreement that would make the whole table decorative. Round 1
  shipped without that function, and the recorder it committed crashed on the missing attribute;
  the failure and its consequences are recorded in AC-13's `Measured today:` rather than glossed.
- **The byte transform a digest was taken at travels with the entry.** Each table row is
  `(strip_width, digest)`, because this issue has contained two stage shapes and an anchored digest
  must keep saying what it said. A generator that silently changed its indentation reddens the
  check instead of quietly re-basing it.

Mutation-falsification design. (a) Hand-edit one `printf` line of a recorded block: that block's
`payload_ac-<n>` reddens and names the digest pair. (b) Delete a probe source: its `source_<file>`
reddens with `missing`. (c) Add a block without recording it: `no_unrecorded_block` reddens, naming
it. (d) Add a stray helper to the probes directory: `no_unrecorded_source` reddens - so the table
gates its own successors, which is also what AC-13 relies on.
  ```bash
AC-9 stage steps begin here: replay_block.py reads this line
- Probes: probes/probe9.py
- Probes: probes/clauses9.py
# AC-9 executes: bash, from the repo root of the tree under test. The two probe files
# above are staged by printf of a quoted line list (T10's staged-probe convention, kept in pure bash so this file's fences stay simple). The probe measures and prints labelled lines; the clause table turns them into one verdict per clause plus a final whole-AC token line. Scratch is _t20_scratch, removed on the way out. rc is diagnostic only - the printed token is the contract.
rm -rf _t20_scratch
test -f backend/app/config.py || { echo "FAIL AC-9: run this block from the repo root of the tree under test"; exit 0; }
test -x backend/.venv/bin/python || { echo "FAIL AC-9: backend/.venv is not linked in this worktree"; exit 0; }
mkdir -p _t20_scratch
T20G=0
export TMPDIR="$PWD/_t20_scratch" TZ=UTC
export T20PROBEPATH="$PWD/backend"
export PYTHONPATH="$T20PROBEPATH"
test -n "$T20PROBEPATH" || { echo "FAIL AC-9: T20PROBEPATH is empty, so the probe could not be told which tree to import"; exit 0; }
export STAFF_PIN=0000
export JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac9aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"
  ```
