  ```bash
   printf '%s\n' 'clause("issue_readable", bool(md) and len(blocks) >= len(EXPECTED_BLOCK_PAYLOADS),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       "the issue must exist and carry every recorded AC block; saw %d of %d (%s)"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       % (len(blocks), len(EXPECTED_BLOCK_PAYLOADS), ",".join(sorted(blocks)) or "none"))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for ac in sorted(EXPECTED_BLOCK_PAYLOADS):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    bodies = blocks.get(ac, [])' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if len(bodies) != 1:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        clause("payload_" + ac.lower(), False,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '               "expected exactly one block for %s in the issue, found %d" % (ac, len(bodies)))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        continue' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    want_strip, want = EXPECTED_BLOCK_PAYLOADS[ac]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    got = digest(normalised(bodies[0], strip=want_strip))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if want == SELF_REFERENTIAL:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # The unpinned entry can still be falsified, and it must be: an entry that can never fail is' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # not a record, it is a decoration. Its block stages this file, so the table'"'"'s bytes are part' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # of the payload - and the ONLY bytes of the table that reach the payload are the digest' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # literals, because every line of this file'"'"'s prose and comments is stripped back to its' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # margin by normalised() before hashing while a payload line keeps everything after its' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # indent. So changing a digest here must change the payload AC-9 stages, and recomputing' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # against a perturbed table catches an entry whose stated reason has stopped being true (for' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # instance because the block stopped staging this file, at which point it should carry a' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # digest and this branch becomes the finding).' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if got == digest(normalised(bodies[0], strip=want_strip), table_perturbed=True):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            clause("payload_" + ac.lower(), False,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                   "%s is pinned as self_referential but its staged payload does not depend on the "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                   "digest table at all, so the entry'"'"'s stated reason no longer holds and the entry "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                   "should carry a real digest" % ac)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            continue' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if want == SELF_REFERENTIAL:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # Deliberately unpinned, and said out loud rather than skipped: this block stages the file' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # that holds this table, so its payload digest is a function of the number it would be' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # compared against and no value can be pinned without changing the bytes it names. The' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # entry stays in the table, with this PASS line, so the gap is a stated fact of the check' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # rather than an AC that quietly stopped being checked - and so that a future edit which' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # breaks the round-1 nine still reddens this same table.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        clause("payload_" + ac.lower(), True,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '               "unpinned: see the entry'"'"'s own comment")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        details.append("PASS AC-9 payload_%s: %s is self_referential (its block stages the file "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                       "holding this table), so its payload is measured by AC-13'"'"'s block-to-block "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                       "meta check rather than by a number here; last honest measurement %s"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                       % (ac.lower(), ac, SELF_REFERENTIAL_STALE))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        continue' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    clause("payload_" + ac.lower(), got == want,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "%s stages payload %s but the recorded digest is %s (stripped at %d): the block and "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "the recorded contract disagree. Regenerate the block with generate_t20_probes.py and "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "commit the table update in the same change" % (ac, got, want, want_strip))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'orphans = sorted(a for a in blocks if a not in EXPECTED_BLOCK_PAYLOADS)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'clause("no_unrecorded_block", not orphans,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       "these blocks exist in the issue with no recorded payload, so nothing verifies what they "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       "stage: %s" % (",".join(orphans) or "none"))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if PROBES.is_dir():' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for name in sorted(EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        p = PROBES / name' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        got = digest(p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) if p.exists() else "missing"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        clause("source_" + name, got == EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS[name],' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '               "%s hashes to %s, recorded %s" % (name, got, EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS[name]))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    extra = sorted(p.name for p in PROBES.iterdir()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                   if p.suffix == ".py" and p.name not in EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                   and p.name not in ("probe9.py", "record_t20_digests.py"))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    clause("no_unrecorded_source", not extra,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "these probe files exist with no recorded digest: %s" % (",".join(extra) or "none"))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # Only the canonical probe/clauses pairs take part in the pairing clause; a directory can' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # legitimately hold a helper with no AC number (a baseline counter, this round'"'"'s mutation' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # builder), and refusing to record one would be a false alarm about a missing half-pair.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    nums = sorted({m.group(1) for m in (re.match(r"(?:probe|clauses)(\d+)\.py", n)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                                        for n in EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS) if m})' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    missing = ["probe%s/clauses%s" % (n, n) for n in nums' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '               if not all((PROBES / ("%s%s.py" % (k, n))).exists()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                          for k in ("probe", "clauses"))]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    clause("probe_clause_pairing", not missing,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "these AC numbers lack one half of their pair: %s" % (",".join(missing) or "none"))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    clause("sources_present", False,' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '           "_docs/issues/_t20/probes/ is absent, so nothing backs the blocks in this tree")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'clause("generator_present", GENERATOR.exists(),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       "%s must exist: it is the only sanctioned way to rebuild a block from these sources"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '       % GENERATOR)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if ok:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("PASS AC-9: every recorded block'"'"'s staged payload matches its digest, every canonical "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "probe matches its digest, and the block-to-probe pairing is complete on both sides "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "(%d blocks, %d sources)" % (len(blocks), len(EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS)))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("FAIL AC-9: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("\n".join(details))' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/probe.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/probe.py never reached disk"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-9 clause table: transposes the digest checker'"'"'s lines into one verdict token.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'The checker at _docs/issues/_t20/probes/probe9.py already prints one PASS/FAIL line per clause and a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'whole-AC verdict, so this table re-derives none of its arithmetic. Re-deriving it would measure the' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'copy of "the payload" sitting in the tree, which is the disagreement AC-9 exists to detect, not a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'restatement of it. What the table adds is the block'"'"'s shell contract: whether the checker produced a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'verdict at all, and whether that verdict survives as the block'"'"'s own token line so a truncating' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'consumer carries the answer even when it never reaches a detail line.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'One addition over a plain transposition, and it is deliberate. The checker prints its whole-AC verdict' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'FIRST and its per-clause detail after it, so the block'"'"'s first line repeats the verdict with the' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'number of unpinned entries inside it. That number matters: an entry pinned as a self-reference is a' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'gap the checker states rather than a clause it measured, and a reader who sees only the first line' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'must learn the gap is there instead of scrolling forty lines to discover that part of the green was' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' pass-by-declaration. >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'lines = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'text = lines.splitlines()' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'verdict = [l for l in text if l.startswith(("PASS AC-9:", "FAIL AC-9:"))]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'fails = [l for l in text if l.startswith("FAIL AC-9:")]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'passes = [l for l in text if l.startswith("PASS AC-9:")]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'unpinned = [l for l in text' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            if l.startswith(("PASS AC-9 ", "FAIL AC-9 ")) and "self_referential" in l]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'pin_count = len([l for l in text if l.startswith("PASS AC-9 payload_")]) - len(unpinned)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if not verdict:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("FAIL AC-9: the digest checker printed no verdict line, so this block measured nothing "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "(its rc is diagnostic only; re-run it from the repo root of the tree under test)")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'elif verdict[0].startswith("PASS AC-9:"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("PASS AC-9: the committed digest checker reports zero drift over %d clause line(s); %d of "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "its recorded block payloads are pinned digests and %d is the block that cannot pin itself "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "(see its own line below)" % (len(passes) - 1, pin_count, len(unpinned)))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("FAIL AC-9: the committed digest checker reports %d failed clause(s), named below"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          % (len(fails) - 1))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("\n".join(l for l in text if l.startswith(("PASS AC-9 ", "FAIL AC-9 "))))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/clauses.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/clauses.py never reached disk"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/probe.py" > "$TMPDIR/out.txt" 2>&1; RC=$?
sed -e "/sqlalchemy.engine/d" -e "/StarletteDeprecation/d" -e "/^  from starlette$/d" "$TMPDIR/out.txt"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/clauses.py" "$TMPDIR/out.txt" | cat; RC2=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
rm -rf _t20_scratch
[ "$RC" = 0 ] || echo "FAIL AC-9: the probe crashed rc=$RC (rc is diagnostic; the verdict token is the contract)"
[ "$RC2" = 0 ] || echo "FAIL AC-9: the clause table could not read the probe output"
AC-9 stage steps end here: replay_block.py reads this line
  ```

    Measured today. The intermediate state is the honest red of a check that had been written but
    never run, and it is quoted before the green because a reader cannot judge a digest table
    without knowing what it refused first. (a) At the intermediate commit, with AC-9's block
    committed and AC-13's region not yet recorded, the block printed `FAIL AC-9: at least one
    clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)` naming `FAIL AC-9 no_unrecorded_source: these probe
    files exist with no recorded digest: clauses13.py,probe13.py` and `FAIL AC-9 payload_ac-13:
    AC-13 stages payload ... but the recorded digest is ...` - the checker refusing to bless a
    table that did not describe the tree it was run in, which is the clause doing its job. (b) At
    the recording commit, with the table regenerated from the same sources the blocks stage, the
    same block prints its `PASS AC-9:` token with every `payload_ac-<n>` and `source_<file>` line
    green; the count in that line is read from the run, not from the recorder's summary. (c) A
    hand-edit to any recorded block's `printf` text turns (b) red and names the digest pair, which
    is the only behaviour this AC is for.

- [ ] **AC-13** Block regeneration has a machine check. The committed generator, recorder and
digest checker under `_docs/issues/_t20/` must be present, importable, in sync with the ten blocks
this file ships, and non-empty on both sides of their digest tables; and this block's own body must
be byte-identical to the block region of the issue it is run against.

Pass: the block prints a line beginning `PASS AC-13:`. Clauses:
`staged_block_is_the_issue_block`, `staged_fence_balanced`, `checker_importable`,
`checker_reports_no_fail`, `machinery_committed`, `digests_pinned_non_empty`.

This is the meta criterion, and it is a **meta** one in the sense that no product change can move
it: fixing `config.py` cannot turn AC-13 green, and hand-editing one line of one block can turn it
red. That inversion is the whole point. Round 1 committed `generate_t20_probes.py`,
`record_t20_digests.py` and `probes/probe9.py` and measured all three in sync by hand at commit
time, and its own shortfall 5 says what that was worth: "neither is wired to an AC here, so a
hand-edit to a block would be caught only by someone thinking to run them." Round 1's byte-identity
claim (block payload equals generated payload) is the *specification* this block implements; it is
not re-derived here, it is now re-checked by machinery on every run.

Design, because two hazards are specific to a self-checking block and both are encoded in the
sources rather than worked around at run time:

- **A block may not contain a code fence.** `probe9.py`'s docstring records why: any line beginning
  with three backticks inside a staged probe would drop an unbalanced fence into this file and break
  every tool that pairs markers to slice blocks out. So AC-13's block stages no new python beyond
  the canonical `probe13.py`/`clauses13.py` pair (it copies its own script file to scratch as the
  text under review, and the two marker lines that say so are stripped on both sides of the
  comparison), and both new sources assemble the fence marker from `chr(96) * 3` instead of
  spelling it. `staged_fence_balanced` is the clause that *requires* this: it asserts the staged
  body holds exactly one bash fence opener and one bare closer, which is the property the hazard
  destroys.
- **The checker is imported, not executed.** Running `probe9.py` as a program from a block would
  require pasting its source (fence hazard, above) or invoking it by path (which the checker would
  then read as a block that does not exist). `runpy.run_path` runs the identical analysis, and this
  block re-emits its PASS/FAIL lines under `CHECK_DETAIL` so QA can read exactly what it said.

Mutation-falsification design, all three states measured (see `Measured today:`). (a) Hand-edit one
printf line in the AC-1 block: `checker_reports_no_fail` reddens by exactly one FAIL line naming the
payload whose digest moved, and `staged_block_is_the_issue_block` stays green because the issue's
AC-13 region is untouched - the pair of answers is the signature of "a block was edited without
regenerating", which is the incident this clause exists to catch. (b) Empty one expected-digest
table: `digests_pinned_non_empty` reddens while the checker itself would happily print PASS, which
is why that clause reads the table rather than trusting the checker's arithmetic. (c) Delete the
recorder: `machinery_committed` reddens.

 (d) A future round adds a block and does not record it: AC-9's `no_unrecorded_block` clause, which
 AC-13 runs and re-emits, reddens - so the meta check also gates the meta check's own successors.
  ```bash
AC-13 stage steps begin here: replay_block.py reads this line
- Probes: probes/probe13.py
- Probes: probes/clauses13.py
# AC-13 executes: bash, from the repo root of the tree under test. The two probe files
# above are staged by printf of a quoted line list (T10's staged-probe convention, kept in pure bash so this file's fences stay simple). The probe measures and prints labelled lines; the clause table turns them into one verdict per clause plus a final whole-AC token line. Scratch is _t20_scratch, removed on the way out. rc is diagnostic only - the printed token is the contract.
rm -rf _t20_scratch
test -f backend/app/config.py || { echo "FAIL AC-13: run this block from the repo root of the tree under test"; exit 0; }
test -x backend/.venv/bin/python || { echo "FAIL AC-13: backend/.venv is not linked in this worktree"; exit 0; }
mkdir -p _t20_scratch
T20G=0
export TMPDIR="$PWD/_t20_scratch" TZ=UTC
export T20PROBEPATH="$PWD/backend"
export PYTHONPATH="$T20PROBEPATH"
test -n "$T20PROBEPATH" || { echo "FAIL AC-13: T20PROBEPATH is empty, so the probe could not be told which tree to import"; exit 0; }
export STAFF_PIN=0000
export JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac13aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"
echo 'import re, sys' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'src = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'head = "AC-13 st" + "age ste" + "ps "' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'off = len(head)' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'mark = re.compile("^" + re.escape(head), re.M)' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'marks = [m.start() for m in mark.finditer(src)]' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'word = "beg" + "in "' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'begs = [k for k in marks if src[k + off:k + off + 7] == word]' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'ends = [k for k in marks if src[k + off:k + off + 5] == "end " and k]' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'region = ""' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'if begs and ends and ends[-1] > begs[0]:' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo '    w = src.index(word, begs[0])' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo '    nl = src.find(chr(10), src.index(":", w))' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo '    region = src[nl + 1:src.rfind(chr(10), 0, ends[-1])] if nl != -1 else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'open(sys.argv[2], "w").write(region)' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'print("SELF_EXTRACTED_BYTES: %d" % len(region))' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'print("SELF_EXTRACT_MATCHED: %s" % ("yes" if region else "no"))' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
echo 'print("SELF_EXTRACT_MARKS: %d" % len(marks))' >> "$TMPDIR/extract13.py"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/extract13.py" "$0" "$TMPDIR/block13.txt"
cp "$TMPDIR/block13.txt" "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-13 meta probe: the block in this issue is the block these sources produce.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'AC-13 is the machine check round 1'"'"'s Constraints shortfall 5 asks for. It boots nothing and' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'measures no product behaviour: it takes the text of ITS OWN stage steps (which the block copied to' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'a scratch file before invoking this probe, with the two marker lines as its delimiters), and it' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'runs the committed digest checker at '"'"'_docs/issues/_t20/probes/probe9.py'"'"' against the real' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' ''"'"'_docs/issues/T20.md'"'"'. Three facts, two of which a digest table alone cannot speak:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  - the staged block body is byte-identical to the block region of the issue, so what was measured' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    is what a reviewer reads (the two marker lines are stripped from both sides before the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    comparison, because their only job is to make the cut possible);' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  - the staged body opens exactly one bash fence and closes it, which is what keeps the issue' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    sliceable by anything that pairs markers;' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  - the committed checker reports zero drift across every recorded block payload and probe source.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Why the checker is imported rather than executed: its own docstring records that a block may not' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'paste its source, because a line beginning with three backticks would drop an unbalanced fence into' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'the issue and break every tool that pairs markers to slice blocks out. The same reason governs this' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'file: the fence marker is assembled from chr(96) here rather than spelled, and the generator'"'"'s' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' ''"'"'chr(96) * 3'"'"' substitution exists so a source can name the marker in prose or code without ever' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'storing it. Importing the checker runs exactly the analysis its own block runs, and this probe' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 're-emits its PASS/FAIL lines so this issue'"'"'s token convention is kept.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import contextlib' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import hashlib' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import io' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import re' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import runpy' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'from pathlib import Path' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The clause-line delimiter, assembled rather than spelled: a literal pipe may not appear in a' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# staged probe, because the tools that slice blocks out of the issue pair fence markers and split' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# arm lines on that character, and this file'"'"'s own output has to survive both.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The code-fence marker, likewise assembled. See the docstring.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'FENCE = chr(96) * 3   # assembled, never spelled: see the stage rule in the generator' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "run"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'OUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'ISSUE = Path("_docs/issues/T20.md")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'PROBE9 = Path("_docs/issues/_t20/probes/probe9.py")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'GENERATOR = Path("_docs/issues/_t20/generate_t20_probes.py")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'RECORD = Path("_docs/issues/_t20/record_t20_digests.py")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The block under review is this file'"'"'s own block: the stage steps the block copied out of the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# running script. Reading them back from that file is what makes the comparison a measurement of' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# the issue rather than a restatement of the generator.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'staged_path = Path(__file__).resolve()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'staged = staged_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") if staged_path.exists() else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'md = ISSUE.read_text(encoding="utf-8") if ISSUE.exists() else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The two lines that bracket a block'"'"'s stage steps. The staged copy carries the opener (the cut' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# starts at it) and not the closer (sed excludes the end line); the issue'"'"'s own region carries' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# neither inside the fence. Stripping whichever markers appear on either side is what makes the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# two comparable without either one editing the bytes being certified.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def strip_markers(text):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """Drop the closing marker line, the one marker a copied region can contain.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    Both sides of the comparison are cut the same way: the opener is excluded from the region on both' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    (a block cannot re-emit a sentence it is forbidden to contain), and the closer is included on both' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    (finding a line and copying it is not the same act as writing it, so the copy carries it). The' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    search is by stem-plus-distinguishing-word rather than by the whole sentence for the same reason' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    the extractor searches that way: this file is staged into the block, so a sentence it held' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    literally would be a sentence the block held, and the block would then contain a third marker.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    stem = "AC-13 st" + "age" + " ste" + "ps "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    out = []' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for ln in text.split("\n"):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        probe = ln.strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if probe.startswith(stem) and probe[:28].count("end ") == 1:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            continue' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        out.append(ln)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return "\n".join(out)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def fence_counts(text):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """How many bash fences a copied region opens and closes, counted as bash and markdown see them.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    The region a block copies out of itself is the BODY between its two marker lines, so the fences' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    that hold it belong to the document rather than to the copy - which is the whole reason this is' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    not a tautology: the count measures whether the body'"'"'s own text keeps the document'"'"'s fence pairing' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    intact. One opener and one closer is the balanced shape, and it is the shape every tool that slices' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    blocks out of the issue depends on.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    Two details make the count honest, and each of them is a bug this function would otherwise have.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    A stage step may QUOTE a fence inside a printf argument, so a fence is only a fence when the line' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    carries nothing else; and a quoted argument spans lines, so a line inside one is not at the top' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    level of the script at all. The quote scan walks the body the way bash reads it - character by' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    character, single and double quotes both, backslash outside single quotes only.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    opens = closes = 0' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    in_single = in_double = False' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    fence = chr(96) * 3' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for ln in text.split("\n"):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        top = 0                          # index where this line stops being inside a quoted argument' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        for i, ch in enumerate(ln):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            if in_single:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                if ch == "'"'"'":' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                    in_single = False' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                    top = i + 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            elif in_double:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                if ch == '"'"'"'"'"':' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                    in_double = False' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                    top = i + 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            elif ch == "'"'"'":' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                in_single = True' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            elif ch == '"'"'"'"'"':' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                in_double = True' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            else:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                top = i + 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        bare = ln[top:].strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if bare == fence + "bash":' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            opens += 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        elif bare == fence:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            closes += 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return opens, closes' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def digest(text):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'region = ""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if md:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # The document'"'"'s own copy of this block'"'"'s stage steps, cut by the same two marker lines the block' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # cut itself by - and cut INDEPENDENTLY, by locating the stem rather than by importing the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # generator'"'"'s constants. If this file used the generator'"'"'s BEGIN/END strings the two sides could' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # agree while both were wrong, which is the one failure mode a self-review must not keep.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    #' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # The cut mirrors the block'"'"'s asymmetry (see the generator'"'"'s note on AC-13'"'"'s extractor): the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # opener is excluded from the region on both sides because a block cannot re-emit it, and the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # closer is INCLUDED on both sides because finding it is not the same as writing it. So the only' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # difference between the two texts is a leading newline, and strip() on each side is the whole' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # normalisation this comparison needs.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # The stem, spelled so that its own concatenation stays split: a source that could match its own' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # stem would match itself before it matched the document (see the generator'"'"'s note on AC-13'"'"'s' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # extractor). The offsets below are len(stem) rather than literals for the same reason one more' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    # time up: rewording the stem must move the address, not leave it reading the wrong characters.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    head = "AC-13 st" + "age ste" + "ps "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    off = len(head)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    marks = [m.start() for m in re.finditer("^" + re.escape(head), md, re.M)]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    word = "beg" + "in "' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    begs = [k for k in marks if md[k + off:k + off + 7] == word]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ends = [k for k in marks if md[k + off:k + off + 5] == "end " and k]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if begs and ends and ends[-1] > begs[0]:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # The same two cuts the block'"'"'s own extractor makes, made again here instead of imported: the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # region is the text between the opener'"'"'s prose tail and the closer'"'"'s own line. Deriving both' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # bounds from the markers - colon, newline - is what lets a rewording move the region rather' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # than cut it in half, and keeping this search independent of the generator'"'"'s constants is' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # what lets the two disagree when one of them is wrong.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        w = md.index(word, begs[0])' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        start = md.find("\n", md.index(":", w)) + 1' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        stop = md.rfind("\n", 0, ends[-1])' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        body = md[start:stop]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # De-indent by the region'"'"'s own indent: in the document the stage steps sit inside a markdown' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # list item and the block'"'"'s copy of itself does not, so the indent is read from the file' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # rather than assumed. Leaving it in would report two identical bodies as different, and a' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        # real change could hide in that noise.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        pad = len(body) - len(body.lstrip())' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        raw = body.split("\n")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        region = "\n".join(ln[pad:] if ln.startswith(" " * pad) else ln' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            for ln in raw).strip("\n")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'opens, closes = fence_counts(staged)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# What the block managed to copy out of itself, printed before anything is compared: when the' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# extraction fails, the failure has to be visible as an extraction rather than as a diff.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("PROBE_SEES_BYTES: %d" % len(staged), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("STAGED_BYTES: %d" % len(staged), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("ISSUE_REGION_BYTES: %d" % len(region), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("STAGED_FENCE_OPENS: %d" % opens, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("STAGED_FENCE_CLOSES: %d" % closes, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("STAGED_MATCHES_ISSUE_REGION: %s"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '      % ("yes" if strip_markers(staged).strip() and strip_markers(staged).strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '         == strip_markers(region).strip() else "no"), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("STAGED_PAYLOAD_DIGEST: %s" % digest(staged), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("ISSUE_REGION_DIGEST: %s" % digest(region), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'buf = io.StringIO()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'note = "imported"' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buf):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        runpy.run_path(str(PROBE9), run_name="_ac13_checker")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except Exception as exc:  # a checker that cannot even load is a finding, not a crash' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    note = "import_error_" + type(exc).__name__' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'report = buf.getvalue()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'verdict = [l for l in report.splitlines() if l.startswith(("PASS AC-9", "FAIL AC-9"))]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("CHECKER: %s" % note, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("CHECKER_VERDICT: %s" % (verdict[0][:220] if verdict else "none"), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("CHECKER_FAIL_COUNT: %d" % len([l for l in report.splitlines() if l.startswith("FAIL ")]),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '      flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("CHECKER_PASS_COUNT: %d" % len([l for l in report.splitlines() if l.startswith("PASS ")]),' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '      flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for ln in report.splitlines():' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if ln.startswith(("PASS ", "FAIL ")):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        print("CHECK_DETAIL " + V + " %s" % ln, flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for path in (GENERATOR, RECORD, PROBE9):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("MACHINERY_PRESENT: %s %s" % (path, "yes" if path.exists() else "no"), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/probe.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/probe.py never reached disk"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-13 clause table: block regeneration has a machine check, and the check is an AC.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Meta criterion. It says nothing about ENV, the reset guard or echo, so no product fix can move it;' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'what it certifies is that this issue'"'"'s own executable contract is tamper-evident. Round 1 committed' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'the machinery (generator, recorder, digest checker) and measured it in sync by hand, and its own' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Constraints shortfall 5 states plainly that nothing in the AC list enforced that. This block is the' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' enforcement. >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Every clause reads probe13.py'"'"'s labelled lines rather than re-deriving anything: a second' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'implementation of "is the table in sync" is exactly the drift this AC exists to miss.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  staged_block_is_the_issue_block - the stage steps the block copied out of itself equal the AC-13' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                                    region of _docs/issues/T20.md, so the measurement names the' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                                    document a reviewer reads.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  staged_fence_balanced           - the staged steps open exactly one bash fence and close it.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  checker_importable              - the committed digest checker loads and prints its verdict.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  checker_reports_no_fail         - the checker'"'"'s FAIL count is zero and it printed PASS lines.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  machinery_committed             - generator, recorder and checker all exist in this tree.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  digests_pinned_non_empty        - the checker'"'"'s expected tables carry entries on BOTH sides,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                                    because an empty expected table is a free green.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import pathlib' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import re' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'import sys' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'lines = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def fact(key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for ln in lines.splitlines():' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if ln.startswith(key + ":"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            return ln.split(":", 1)[1].strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def count(key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    try:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return int(fact(key))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    except ValueError:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return None' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'staged_bytes = count("STAGED_BYTES")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'region_bytes = count("ISSUE_REGION_BYTES")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'opens = count("STAGED_FENCE_OPENS")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'closes = count("STAGED_FENCE_CLOSES")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'fail_count = count("CHECKER_FAIL_COUNT")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'pass_count = count("CHECKER_PASS_COUNT")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'machinery = [ln for ln in lines.splitlines() if ln.startswith("MACHINERY_PRESENT:")]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'missing = [ln.split()[1] for ln in machinery if ln.rstrip().endswith("no")]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'verdict = fact("CHECKER_VERDICT")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The pinned tables are counted out of the checker'"'"'s own source rather than taken from its' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# arithmetic: a checker whose expected table had been emptied still prints a PASS, and non-empty is' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# a property of the file, so the clause reads the file. An entry is a value, so a digest quoted in' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# a comment does not count toward the total.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    src = pathlib.Path("_docs/issues/_t20/probes/probe9.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    cut = src.index("EXPECTED_SOURCE_DIGESTS")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    blocks_pinned = len(re.findall(r'"'"'"AC-\d+": \(\d, "[0-9a-f]"'"'"' + "{16}" + r'"'"'"\)'"'"', src[:cut]))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    sources_pinned = len(re.findall(r'"'"'"[^"]+\.py": "[0-9a-f]"'"'"' + "{16}" + r'"'"'"\)'"'"', src[cut:]))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except Exception:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    blocks_pinned, sources_pinned = 0, 0' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'CHECKS = [' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("staged_block_is_the_issue_block",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     bool(staged_bytes) and staged_bytes > 400 and bool(region_bytes)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     and fact("STAGED_MATCHES_ISSUE_REGION") == "yes",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the stage steps the block copied out of itself must exist and equal the AC-13 block region "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "of _docs/issues/T20.md (marker lines excepted), so this block reviews the file under review; "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "saw: staged=%s region=%s match=%s digests=%s/%s"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (staged_bytes, region_bytes, fact("STAGED_MATCHES_ISSUE_REGION") or "none",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        fact("STAGED_PAYLOAD_DIGEST") or "none", fact("ISSUE_REGION_DIGEST") or "none")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("staged_fence_balanced",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     opens == 1 and closes == 1,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the staged stage steps open exactly one bash fence and close it, so anything that pairs "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "markers can slice this block out again; saw: opens=%s closes=%s" % (opens, closes)),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("checker_importable",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     fact("CHECKER") == "imported" and verdict.startswith(("PASS AC-9", "FAIL AC-9")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the committed digest checker must load and print its own verdict line; saw: %s / %s"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (fact("CHECKER") or "none", verdict or "none")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("checker_reports_no_fail",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     fail_count == 0 and bool(pass_count),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the committed digest checker must report zero FAIL lines and at least one PASS line; drift "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "names itself in the CHECK_DETAIL lines above; saw: fail=%s pass=%s"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (fail_count, pass_count)),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("machinery_committed",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     len(machinery) == 3 and not missing,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the generator, the recorder and the checker must all be present, because the Definition of "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "Done routes every block change through them; saw missing: %s"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (",".join(missing) or "none")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("digests_pinned_non_empty",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     blocks_pinned > 0 and sources_pinned > 0,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the checker'"'"'s expected tables must pin entries for both block payloads and probe sources; "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "an empty table is a green that costs nothing to keep; saw: blocks=%d sources=%d"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (blocks_pinned, sources_pinned)),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' ']' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'ok = True' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'details = []' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for name, good, detail in CHECKS:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if good:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        details.append("PASS AC-13 %s" % name)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    else:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        ok = False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        details.append("FAIL AC-13 %s: %s" % (name, detail))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'if ok:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("PASS AC-13: the staged stage steps are byte-identical to the issue'"'"'s AC-13 region with a "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "balanced fence, the committed digest checker imports and reports zero drift, and its "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          "pinned tables cover %d block payloads and %d probe sources"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '          % (blocks_pinned, sources_pinned))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' else: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    print("FAIL AC-13: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("\n".join(details))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
test -s "$TMPDIR/clauses.py" || echo "FAIL: TMPDIR/clauses.py never reached disk"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/probe.py" > "$TMPDIR/out.txt" 2>&1; RC=$?
sed -e "/sqlalchemy.engine/d" -e "/StarletteDeprecation/d" -e "/^  from starlette$/d" "$TMPDIR/out.txt"
backend/.venv/bin/python "$TMPDIR/clauses.py" "$TMPDIR/out.txt" | cat; RC2=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
rm -rf _t20_scratch
[ "$RC" = 0 ] || echo "FAIL AC-13: the probe crashed rc=$RC (rc is diagnostic; the verdict token is the contract)"
[ "$RC2" = 0 ] || echo "FAIL AC-13: the clause table could not read the probe output"
AC-13 stage steps end here: replay_block.py reads this line
  ```

    Measured today. The three falsification states are what make this clause worth its weight, so
    all four rows are real measurements, each taken from the repo root with `runacs.py` or the
    equivalent single-block run: - At base `bad4f98`, i.e. before this round recorded anything:
    `FAIL AC-13: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)`, `rc=0`, with `FAIL AC-13
    checker_reports_no_fail: ... saw: fail=16 pass=26` and `FAIL AC-13 digests_pinned_non_empty:
    ... saw: blocks=5 sources=10`. Those sixteen FAIL lines are the round-1 trust anchor meeting
    the round-2 file, and they name themselves exactly: five `payload_ac-N` lines (the recorded
    tables carry no entry for a ten-block issue), `no_unrecorded_block: these blocks exist in the
    issue with no recorded payload ... AC-10,AC-11,AC-12,AC-13`, and ten `source_probeN.py` /
    `source_clausesN.py` lines for the new probe files. This is the honest red of a meta criterion
    whose pinned state had not been updated, and it is the red that the recording commit below
    exists to retire. - At this round's recording commit (probes and blocks generated from the
    same sources, then `record_t20_digests.py` run once): `PASS AC-13: the staged block body is
    byte-identical to the issue's AC-13 region with a balanced fence, the committed digest checker
    imports and reports zero drift, and its pinned tables cover 10 block payloads and 18 probe
    sources`, with `CHECKER_FAIL_COUNT: 0` and all six AC-13 clauses green. This is the state
    AC-13 is expected to hold at the fix commit, and it is the state whose green this round
    verifies once. - Hand-edit mutant (one `printf` line of the AC-1 block changed by one
    character, sources untouched): `FAIL AC-13 ... saw: fail=1 pass=<n>`, the single FAIL naming
    `payload_ac-1: AC-1 stages payload <digest> but the recorded digest is <other>: the block and
    the recorded contract disagree. Regenerate the block with generate_t20_probes.py and commit
    the table update in the same change`, while `staged_block_is_the_issue_block` stays green.
    That asymmetry is the signature of an unrecorded hand-edit, and it is the answer this whole
    clause was written to produce. - Deleted-machinery mutant (`record_t20_digests.py` renamed
    away): `FAIL AC-13 machinery_committed: ... saw missing:
    _docs/issues/_t20/record_t20_digests.py`.

- [ ] **AC-10** The two documents an operator reads stop documenting the fail-open: `README.md`'s
backend variable table and `_docs/deployment.md`'s environment table must both carry `ENV` as a
**required** variable with no permissive default and must name the whole allow-list
`development / test / production`, and `deployment.md`'s deployment instruction must stop telling
the operator to "set `ENV=production`" without saying the variable is mandatory and what the other
two values mean.

Pass: the block prints a line beginning `PASS AC-10:`. Clauses: `readme_env_row_required`,
`deploy_env_row_required`, `no_bare_production_instruction`,
`no_surviving_optional_default_claim`. All four are greps over the shipped documents; the first
three are this AC's red at the groom base and the fourth is a green pin on the parts of the
documents that are already correct and must stay correct.

Why a document is worth an executable check at all: A-1 names the docs as part of the exposure,
and D-1's contract change is invisible to anyone who reads only `README.md`, where the row says
`ENV / development / no / Environment` today. A fix that edits `backend/app/config.py` and leaves
that row untouched repairs the process and keeps publishing the old promise, and the next round
that reads the row restores the default with a straight face - which is how a documented default
became a security defect in the first place. The rows are tab-delimited in both files (measured,
not assumed: `awk 'NR==275' README.md | cat -A` shows `ENV^Idevelopment^Ino^IEnvironment$`), so
the probe reads cells rather than trusting a Markdown parser that is not installed.

Scope discipline, stated because it is load-bearing: **this block owns the document half only.**
It boots nothing and runs no suite. AC-6 (round 1's deferred number, not written here) would own
"the documented boot still works"; conflating the two would let a stale row and a broken suite
hide in one verdict. The AC-9 clause `no_unrecorded_block` additionally forbids a docs block that
did not make it into the digest table, so this block cannot quietly escape the meta check.

Mutation-falsification design. (a) Land the code fix and leave both rows: `readme_env_row_required`
and `deploy_env_row_required` stay FAIL, which is this clause pair's purpose. (b) Flip only the
Required cell (`ENV / development / yes / Environment`): the default cell still names a permissive
environment and the clause refuses it, because "required" next to a documented default is exactly
the ambiguity that produced the defect. (c) Name only `production` in the row: the allow-list test
refuses it, since an operator who needs the dev reset cannot find it in the document. (d) Write
prose elsewhere that still calls `ENV` optional: `no_surviving_optional_default_claim` counts it.
  ```bash
AC-10 stage steps begin here: replay_block.py reads this line
- Probes: probes/probe10.py
- Probes: probes/clauses10.py
# AC-10 executes: bash, from the repo root of the tree under test. The two probe files
# above are staged by printf of a quoted line list (T10's staged-probe convention, kept in pure bash so this file's fences stay simple). The probe measures and prints labelled lines; the clause table turns them into one verdict per clause plus a final whole-AC token line. Scratch is _t20_scratch, removed on the way out. rc is diagnostic only - the printed token is the contract.
rm -rf _t20_scratch
test -f backend/app/config.py || { echo "FAIL AC-10: run this block from the repo root of the tree under test"; exit 0; }
test -x backend/.venv/bin/python || { echo "FAIL AC-10: backend/.venv is not linked in this worktree"; exit 0; }
mkdir -p _t20_scratch
T20G=0
export TMPDIR="$PWD/_t20_scratch" TZ=UTC
export T20PROBEPATH="$PWD/backend"
export PYTHONPATH="$T20PROBEPATH"
test -n "$T20PROBEPATH" || { echo "FAIL AC-10: T20PROBEPATH is empty, so the probe could not be told which tree to import"; exit 0; }
export STAFF_PIN=0000
export JWT_SECRET="t20groom-ac10aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-10 probe: the operator-facing documents stop documenting the fail-open.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '                                or defaults to development.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '# a block stages are exactly these bytes, so the check has to deny itself too.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'DEPLOY = Path("_docs/deployment.md")' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def read(path):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") if path.exists() else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def env_row(text):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """The ENV row of a tab-delimited variable table, or the empty string."""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for ln in text.splitlines():' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if re.match(r"^\s*ENV\s+\S", ln):' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            return ln.strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return ""' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'readme = read(README)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'print("README_ENV_ROW: %s" % env_row(readme), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("DEPLOY_ENV_ROW: %s" % env_row(deploy), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The bare production instruction: a line that only tells the operator to set one value.' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'weak = [ln.strip() for ln in deploy.splitlines()' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if re.match(r"^\s*[-*]?\s*Set ['"'"'\"]?ENV=production['"'"'\"]?\.?\s*$", ln)]' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'print("DEPLOY_ENV_LINE: %s" % (weak[0] if weak else ""), flush=True)' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The "not a table cell" separator is the pipe character, assembled rather than spelled:' >> "$TMPDIR/probe.py"

   printf '%s\n' '    operator can choose without reading source.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    """' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    c = cells(row)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if len(c) < 4 or c[0] != "ENV":' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if c[2].lower() not in ("yes", "true", "required"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if c[1].strip().lower() in ("development", "test", "production", "", "none", "-", "(none)"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    if any(v not in row for v in ("development", "test", "production")):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        return False' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return True' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' 'row_readme = fact("README_ENV_ROW")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'row_deploy = fact("DEPLOY_ENV_ROW")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'line_deploy = fact("DEPLOY_ENV_LINE")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' try: >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    claims = int(fact("DOCS_DEFAULT_CLAIM_COUNT"))' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'except ValueError:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    claims = None' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'clauses = [' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("readme_env_row_required",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     row_is_required_no_default(row_readme),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "the README'"'"'s ENV row must mark the variable required, carry no permissive default and name "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "development|test|production (row shape: ENV, a non-environment default cell, yes, then a description); saw: %r"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (row_readme or "no ENV row in README.md")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("deploy_env_row_required",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     row_is_required_no_default(row_deploy),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "deployment.md'"'"'s ENV row must carry the same required/no-default/allow-list shape; saw: %r"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (row_deploy or "no ENV row in _docs/deployment.md")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("no_bare_production_instruction",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     line_deploy == "",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "deployment.md must not carry a bare '"'"'Set ENV=production.'"'"' instruction that names one value "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "without saying the variable is required and what the other values mean; saw: %r"' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     % (line_deploy or "none")),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    ("no_surviving_optional_default_claim",' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     claims == 0,' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "zero lines in README.md or deployment.md may still describe ENV as optional or as defaulting "' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '     "to development; saw: %s" % ("ARM MISSING" if claims is None else claims)),' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' ']' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'ok = True' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'details = []' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'for name, good, detail in clauses:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
  ```

    Measured today (this block, run as-is at base `bad4f98`, i.e. before any AC-10 fix; verdict
    line `FAIL AC-10: at least one clause failed (see the FAIL lines below)`, `rc=0`, and a red
    block exiting 0 is the correct shape because the token is the contract): `README_ENV_ROW:
    ENV<tab>development<tab>no<tab>Environment`, `DEPLOY_ENV_ROW:
    ENV<tab>development<tab>no<tab>development / test / production`, `DEPLOY_ENV_LINE: - Set
    \`ENV=production\`.`, `DOCS_DEFAULT_CLAIM_COUNT: 0`, and the clause lines `FAIL AC-10
    readme_env_row_required: ... saw: 'ENV\tdevelopment\tno\tEnvironment'` (README:275, the row
    the orch annotation located), `FAIL AC-10 deploy_env_row_required: ... saw:
    'ENV\tdevelopment\tno\tdevelopment / test / production'` (deployment.md:112 - a row round 1
    had not named, found by reading the document instead of the annotation), `FAIL AC-10
    no_bare_production_instruction: ... saw: '- Set \`ENV=production\`.'` (deployment.md:169) and
    `PASS AC-10 no_surviving_optional_default_claim` as today's green pin: no line anywhere in the
    two files uses the *words* "optional" or "defaults to development", which is precisely why the
    grep that matters is the one over the row shape and not one over prose. Raw greps for the same
    three refusals, run standalone at base from the repo root and printed here so a reviewer can
    reproduce each clause without the block (`rc=0` means the bad row is still there): `grep -nE
    '^[[:space:]]*ENV[[:space:]]+development[[:space:]]+no([[:space:]]|$)' README.md` -> `275:ENV
    development no Environment`, rc=0; the same expression against `_docs/deployment.md` ->
    `112:ENV development no development / test / production`, rc=0; `grep -nE
    '^[[:space:]]*[-*]?[[:space:]]*Set [`"]?ENV=production[`"]?\.?[[:space:]]*$'
    _docs/deployment.md` -> `169:- Set \`ENV=production\`.`, rc=0; and the surviving-claim grep
    `grep -niE 'ENV[^|]{0,40}(optional|defaults?[[:space:]]+(to[[:space:]]+)?["\x27]?development)'
    README.md _docs/deployment.md` -> no output, rc=1, matching the PASS line above. Green-shape
    rehearsal, measured against a tree carrying the two flipped rows and the rewritten deployment
    instruction (`ENV / (none - required) / yes / <text naming all three values>` in both tables):
    the block prints `PASS AC-10: README.md and _docs/deployment.md both document ENV as required

    required-row patterns.

- [ ] **AC-11** No second opt-in gate exists and none may be invented: in a process booted
`ENV=production` or `ENV=test`, exporting every plausible second-gate name
(`ALLOW_DATA_RESET`, `TQ_ALLOW_RESET`, `REQUIRE_RESET_TOKEN`, `RESET_ALLOWED`, `DEV_MODE`,
`FEATURE_DEV_MODE`, `RESET_CONFIRM`) leaves the reset refused, and in a process with no `ENV` at
all no such variable arms it either. `ENV=development` with **nothing** exported still answers 204,
so the documented dev reset is not secretly gated behind a flag nobody documented; and `SQL_ECHO`
stays the only exported variable that moves engine echo.

Pass: the block prints a line beginning `PASS AC-11:`. Clauses: `p_absent_arm_no_reset`,
`p_offlist_label_never_204`, `p_control_test_label_403`, `p_explicit_development_204`,
`p_only_sql_echo_arms_echo`. One subprocess boot per arm (25 arms), because the question is what
an *environment* can do, and that is a process fact.

Design in prose first, as this AC was assigned: **D-2 refuses a second opt-in variable, and the
refusal has to be falsifiable or it is a preference, not a contract.** Two wrong answers are
possible after the fix, and they are not the same wrong answer. (1) A fix that keeps the env check
and adds `and os.environ.get("ALLOW_DATA_RESET") == "true"` is a second default one level down -
precisely D-2's stated reason - and in the field it reads as "the operator just has to export one
more thing", which is the mental state that produced A-3. (2) A fix that *replaces* the env check
with such a flag is worse: it un-arms the guard in exactly the named-permissive environment while
looking like defence-in-depth. AC-2 cannot see either, because AC-2 asks "does the documented
development reset still work" and both mutants answer yes; AC-1 cannot see them either, because
both mutants keep D-1's validator. This block is what they trip on.

The arms are therefore: the no-ENV arm bare and with every candidate flag at once (the clause
accepts a refused boot or a 403, never a 204, and never a 404 - a request that never reached the
guard is not a refusal), each candidate flag alone under `ENV=production` and alone under
`ENV=test` (the `test` set is the control that the guard is not merely broken everywhere), and
`ENV=development` bare plus each flag (the reset must still work with nothing exported, and none of
the flags may arm echo). `SQL_ECHO=true` under `development` is the one arm where echo *must*
appear, which is what keeps this clause pair from being satisfiable by deleting the flag AC-5
requires.

Measured red at base, honestly scoped: the base tree has no D-1 gate, so today's no-ENV arm boots
as development and answers 204 with or without any flag exported - that is this AC's own red, and
it is the A-3 wipe reached through the no-ENV door. The four remaining clauses are GREEN at base
today (production and `test` answer 403 to every invented flag because the flag means nothing at
all, and development answers 204) and are expected to stay green across the fix; the block is not
red everywhere, and saying so is the point of naming each clause.

Mutation-falsification design. (a) Add `and os.environ.get("ALLOW_DATA_RESET") == "true"` to the
guard: `p_explicit_development_204` reddens, because the documented development call now needs an
undocumented export. (b) Replace the guard with a flag check: `p_offlist_label_never_204` reddens
(the production-plus-flag arm answers 204). (c) Delete `SQL_ECHO` while "hardening":
`p_only_sql_echo_arms_echo` reddens. (d) Accept 404 as a refusal: impossible here by construction,
since every refusal clause names 403 or a refused boot and prints `RESET:` verbatim.

  ```bash
first=:
   printf '%s\n' '"""T20 AC-11 clause table: no second opt-in gate re-arms the wipe or the echo.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'Reads the probe'"'"'s labelled ARM lines. Four clauses:' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  p_absent_arm_no_reset   - the no-ENV arm never hands out a 204. Today the bare no-ENV' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            process answers 204 (A-3 reproduced); after D-1 it refuses to' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            boot, and adding invented opt-in variables to that arm must not' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            change either answer.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  p_offlist_label_never_204 - every '"'"'production'"'"' arm answers 403 or never boots, whatever' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            second-gate variable travels with it. This is the clause a fix' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            that re-keys the guard on '"'"'os.environ["ALLOW_DATA_RESET"]'"'"' fails.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '                            guard that merely broke everywhere cannot satisfy this AC.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '  p_explicit_development_204 - the documented reset still works with NOTHING exported beyond' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            ENV=development and the documented body confirmation, and it is' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '                            not secretly gated behind a flag nobody documented.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '"""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# The arm line delimiter, assembled from a character rather than spelled: a literal pipe' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# may not appear in a staged probe, because the tools that slice blocks out of the issue' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '# pair fence markers and split arm lines on that character.' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'V = chr(124)' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'lines = open(sys.argv[1]).read() if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] else ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'arms = {}' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"

   printf '%s\n' '         "DEV_MODE", "FEATURE_DEV_MODE", "RESET_CONFIRM"]' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'ECHO_OFF_BY_LABEL = ("production", "test")' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def field(name, key):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    for chunk in arms.get(name, "").split(" ; "):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '        if chunk.startswith(key + ":"):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '            return chunk.split(":", 1)[1].strip()' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '    return ""' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' '' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
   printf '%s\n' 'def booted(name):' >> "$TMPDIR/clauses.py"
  ```
